     2. A.   12.15.15 
2.   RECOGNITION  
A.   “Flags for Classrooms Program” - 18th District Sons of the 
       American Legion 
Tonight, we are pleased to welcome members of the 18th District Sons 
of the American Legion who have committed themselves to helping 
our veterans, troops and communities in need. One of their goals is to 
make sure there is  an American Flag placed in every classroom in 
Oakland County.  Mr. Randy Jackson, Chairman for the “Flags for 
Classrooms Program”, is here with us this evening to share a little about 
this program.  Following Mr. Jackson’s presentation, flag carriers of the 
18th District Sons of the American Legion will present 62 American flags 
to Mr. Mark Dziatczak, Deputy Superintendent for Teaching and 
Learning, who w ill be accepting them on behalf of the District .  These 
flags will be placed in designated classrooms at Baker, Boulan Park and 
Larson Middle Schools as well as Athens and Troy High.   
Recommended Action:  Invite Mr. Jackson up to speak for a few 
minutes and then flag carriers will present the flags to Mark 
Dziatczak. 
3
     2. B.   12.15.15 
2.   RECOGNITION  
B.   TSD Mission Moment—Wattles and Troy Union WATCH D.O.G.S. 
Tonight, we recognize the Wattles and Troy Union WATCH D.O.G.S.  
The WATCH D.O.G.S, which stands for Dads of Great Students, are fathers, 
grandfathers, uncles, and other father-figures who volunteer at least one full day 
during the school year. These “WatchDOGS” work right along side educators as 
they read and work on flash cards with students, play at recess, eat lunch with 
students, watch t he school entrances and hallways, assist with traffic flow and 
any other assigned activities where they actively engage with not only their own 
students, but other students as well. 
At this time, we would like to invite the following individuals up to explain the 
goals of the WATCH D.O.G.S program as well as share more information about 
how it works in our schools. At the conclusion of their presentation, we invi te the 
participants to come up and  receive a certificate and personal recognition from 
our Board: 
Joyce Brasington, Wattles Principal 
Mike Cottone, Troy Union Interim Principal 
Brad Sassack, Wattles 2nd Grade Teacher 
Jamie VanHouzen, Troy Union Physical Education Teacher 
Patrick Werner, Troy Union 5th Grade Teacher 
Brian Corbett, Troy Union Parent 
Bob Schneeweis, Wattles Parent 
Brendan Sennott, Troy Union Parent 
Michael Upton, Wattles Parent (tentative) 
________________________________________________ 
Recommended Action:  Invite participants up and present certificates.

RECOGNITION 
TSD Mission Moment – Larson 
which was the Larson Stream Leaders Program.  He 
invited Joe Duda, Principal of Larson Middle School to 
introduce the program along with teacher Sandy 
for the Stream Leaders Program at Larson.  Ms. 
Bammel explained that Stream Leaders is a program 
sponsored by the Clinton River Watershed Council that 
provides students the oppo rtunity to participate in 
water quality monitoring, data interpretation, and 
citizen action within their community. The program 
began at Larson in the fall of 2013 and continues to 
students at Larson participate in the program.  Four 8 th 
grade science students were in attendance at this 
evening’s meeting – Sabrina Badia, Rosemary 
Northcote, Audrey Parks, and Alexa Withun.  They each 
shared information about their involvement and 
participation in the program.  The showed a brief 
PowerPoint presentation, which included a link to a 
short video showing students who participated in the 
program.   The Board inquired about the outcome of 
the testing results obtain ed from their study and 
suggested they be put in the School Life paper.  
Following the presentation, each student was presented 
with a certificate of recognition.

TROY SCHOOL DISTRICT 
TROY, MICHIGAN 48098 
ANNUAL SUMMER TAX RESOLUTION 
WHEREAS, this Board of Education by resolution of 
November 17, 2015, determined to impose a summer 
property tax levy to collect one-half of school property 
taxes, including debt services, upon property located 
within the Troy School District, beginning with 2016 
and continuing from year to  year until specifically 
revoked by this Board of Education. 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED THAT: 
1.  This Board of Education, pursuant to 1976 P.A. 451, 
as amended, hereby invokes a summer tax levy of one-
half of school property taxes, including debt service, 
continuing with 2016, and continuing from year to year

OTHER 
FIRST READING:  Revisions to 
Board Policy E-1102 Use of 
School Buildings and Facilities  
Members of the Board Policy Committee have met to 
review Board Policy E-1102, Use of School Buildings and 
Facilities. The policy has been reviewed by the Board of 
Education’s attorney to make sure it complies with State 
law, the Open Meetings Act, and the Revised School 
Code.  All Board members have read this proposed 
revised policy and will be aske d to review it a second 
time prior to the next Board meeting, which will be held 
on December 15, 2015.  The Board will be asked to 
vote on adoption of these revisions under the consent 
agenda at the December meeting. 
FIRST READING:  New Board 
Policy D-1401 Reimbursement 
of Board Member or Employee 
Expenses 
Members of the Board Policy Committee have met to 
review the proposed new Board Policy D-1401, 
Reimbursement of Board Member or Employee 
Expenses. The policy has been reviewed by the Board 
of Education’s attorney to make sure it complies with 
State law, the Open Meetings Act, and the Revised 
School Code.  All Board members have read this 
proposed new policy and will be asked to review it a 
second time prior to the ne xt Board meeting, which will 
be held on December 15,  2015.  The Board will be 
asked to vote on adoption of this policy under the 
consent agenda at the December meeting.

USE OF SCHOOL PROPERTY     E-1100 
E-1102 USE OF SCHOOL BUILDINGS AND FACILITIES 
 The superintendent may approve the use of school facilities according to 
the schedule of fees approved by the Board.  The superintendent, or 
designee, establishes such rules and regulations needed to implement 
this policy as well as assure the preservation of school district property. 
school community.  Therefore, it is the policy of the Board to encourage 
the use of the facilities by established local groups and organizations 
whose purpose and objectives contribute to the school program or to the 
community. 
The Board also recognizes that the primary purpose of the district’s 
facilities is to implement the instructional program of the District.  The 
Board further recognizes that there are costs involved in the non-school 
use of the facilities and that charges should be made so that taxpayer 
funds are not used in support of non-school activities. 
The Superintendent/Designee shall develop a fee schedule and 
regulations governing the use and rental of district facilities to protect 
the regular instructional program, those who use the facilities, and the 
taxpayers who have provided the facilities.  These regulations shall 
define those groups permitted to use district facilities as well as the 
specific limitations and prohibited activities in school facilities.  No 
person or organization shall be granted permission to use the district 
facilities who has not agreed in writing to be bound by the regulations of 
the District. 
In case of disaster or emergencies in the area of the School District or in 
nearby areas, the Superintendent may open district facilities and sites to 
assist civil authorities in the relief of human misery and the saving of 
life. 
Formatted: Indent: First line:  0"
Please reference Policy K-1501 for Visitors To The Schools.

REIMBURSEMENT OF EXPENSES   D-1400 
D-1401 REIMBURSEMENT OF BOARD MEMBER OR EMPLOYEE EXPENSES 
The Board of Education recognizes that there are reasonable expenses incurred 
by the employees and board members on behalf of the District when carrying 
out the business of the District.  Board members and employees shall be 
reimbursed for actual and necessary expenses incurred in discharging their 
official duties and in performing functions authorized by the Board. 
A.  Schedule of Pre-Approved Reimbursable Expenses 
The Board designates the following specific categories of duties and functions 
which are pre-approved as reimbursable expenses:  
(excluding out-of-state conference for Board of Education members – 
reference policy B-2001 and section B below). 
2. County and regional education-related associations of inter-governmental 
meetings. 
3. Education or civic programs including materials and supplies. 
4. Education-related seminars, continuing education classes, workshops and 
educational programs of other states and countries. 
5. Expenses include registration fees, costs of travel, lodging and meals where 
these expenses are directly related to attendance at the conference, seminar, 
class, workshop or educational program of other states or countries. 
6. The cost of travel includes air travel at coach rate, automobile mileage at 
the Internal Revenue Service appropriate rate per mile, parking tolls, taxi 
or rental car allowances at the current prevailing rates for travel outside the 
District, when necessary, to attend a function. 
7. Other expenses related to the professional development of the Board 
member or employee. These expenses include, but are not limited to, 
professional reading materials, books, audio tapes and visual materials 
which enhance the professional development of the Board member or 
employee.  
8. Telephone calls, facsimile expenses and other applicable communication 
expenses. 
9. Other expenses related to carrying out the business of the District in which 
the Board of Education, Superintendent or designee feel is reasonable and 
within the parameters of the adopted budget. 
127
1. Local, state and national educational conferences and business meetings 
Formatted: Font: Palatino Linotype, Not Bold
B. Non-Scheduled Categories of Expenses. 
If a Board member or employee incurs an expense for an official duty or 
function not appearing on the schedule of pre-approved reimbursable 
expenses, the Board shall not reimburse that expense unless a motion to 
approve the non-scheduled expense is adopted before the expense is incurred.  
This includes out-of-state meetings of Board members that must have prior 
approval by resolution (reference policy B-2001).  
C. Expense Vouchers 
To receive reimbursement, a Board member or employee shall submit to the 
District’s business office an expense voucher and supporting receipts or other 
evidence to verify each expense incurred. 
D. Spouses and Other Family Members 
Expenses for spouses accompanying Board members or employees in 
discharging their official duties or performing authorized functions are not 
reimbursable. 
E. Budget 
Funds shall be budgeted on an annual basis for Board members and 
employees to attend and participate in the meetings, workshops, associations, 
conferences and other activities described in this bylaw. 
F. Procedures 
The Superintendent or designee is authorized to develop procedures for 
reimbursement for expenses incurred by members of the Board of Education 
or employee in accordance with this bylaw. 
Legal Reference:  Revised School Code MCL 380.1254

2015-16 GF Budget Amendment #1 Expenses 
 Salaries have remained consistent with original budget 
 Benefits increase of 5% is due to the $2.1 million increase in MPSERS UAAL 
(offset by revenue) 
 Purchased services slight increase of 2% 
 Supplies decrease of 7% a result of decreased the textbook line items by $442,000 
 Capital Outlay decrease of 25% a result of decreasing budgeted line items. 
 Other and Outgoing Transfer and Other Transactions were not effected greatly in 
this amendment 
 Removed the $3,000,000 budget reduction assumption and included a $1,500,000 
positive variance assumption in the budget amendment.

Budget Process & Timeline  
February Governor's Executive Budget
March - April Individual Administration Budget Meetings
February - May Develop and Confirm Budget Assumptions
March - June Develop Budget
May/June Board Meeting Public Hearing and Millage Rate Adoption
June Board Meeting Budget Adoption

Special Revenue Funds will be amended as needed throughout the rest of the fiscal year.

C.  CONSIDERATION:  AUTHORIZATION TO SEEK BIDS FOR 
PROPERTY/CASUALTY/ LIABILITY 
INSURANCE FOR 2016/2017 
The administration recommends that Troy School District, as a 
member of the MAISL Insurance Pool, provide notice of withdrawing 
from the Trust in order to consider additional bids for 
property/casualty/liability insurance beginning July 1, 2016. 
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by:   
 Supported by: 
 Be it therefore resolved  that the Troy Board of Education approve 
notification to MAISL of withdrawal from the Trust in order to consider 
additional bids for insurance beginning July 1, 2016. 
 Troy School District understands that withdrawal from the pool shall be in 
accordance with MAISL by -laws, and under the terms and conditions 
established by the MAISL Pool Board of Directors.  This resolution sh all be 
submitted in order to be in compliance with MAISL by -laws and does not 
constitute a final decision on this matter.

D.  CONSIDERATION:  Roofing Designer/Consultant 
An RFP was issued earlier this year to obtain competitive bids for Roof 
Assessment and Consulting Services .  Bid responses were reviewed by 
Mr. Rob Carson, Director of Maintenance and Operations  and Michelle 
Kerns, Owners Representative, and the work for the current year roof 
assessment was awarded previously.  The bid included assessment, 
consulting and design services through 2019.  I t is recommended the 
bid be awarded as stated below.  
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by:   
 Supported by: 
 Be it therefore resolved  that the Troy Board of Education authorize 
administration to approve the contract with WeatherTech Consulting 
Group, Inc. for roof assessment, design and consulting services through 
2019, consistent with their May 21, 2015 bid submission  and that these 
services be approved as an expenditure of the 2014 Capital Projects Fund.
